# OSDC — 2026-10-05

> 33 advisories analyzed

---

## GHSA-3vgf-8m4q-q4qr · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox environment failed to properly protect the prototypes of host TypedArray and ArrayBuffer intrinsics, as well as various iterator prototypes. These prototypes were not included in the list of protected host objects, allowing sandbox code to modify their host-realm definitions.

### Impact

An attacker could mutate host TypedArray and ArrayBuffer intrinsics, potentially leading to arbitrary code execution or other severe integrity violations outside the sandbox.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/bridge.js
+++ b/lib/bridge.js
@@ -31,6 +31,29 @@ const globalsList = [
 	'WeakSet',
 	'Promise',
 	'Function',
+	'ArrayBuffer',
+	'SharedArrayBuffer',
+	'DataView',
+	'Uint8Array',
+	'Int8Array',
+	'Uint8ClampedArray',
+	'Uint16Array',
+	'Int16Array',
+	'Uint32Array',
+	'Int32Array',
+	'Float32Array',
+	'Float64Array',
+	'BigInt64Array',
+	'BigUint64Array',
 ];
 
 const errorsList = [
@@ -79,6 +102,54 @@ try {
 	thisGlobalPrototypes['AsyncGeneratorFunction'] = eval('(async function*() {})').constructor.prototype;
 } catch (e) {}
 
+// SECURITY (GHSA-3vgf-8m4q-q4qr / GHSA-59g5-pmg6-5gr4): abstract intrinsic
+// prototypes with NO named global. Same protection gap as the binary-data
+// globals above, but they cannot be reached through `global[key]`, so resolve
+// them structurally (mirroring the AsyncFunction / GeneratorFunction pattern).
+// Adding them to `thisGlobalPrototypes` routes them into `protectedHostObjects`
+// (which enumerates every key) and the mapping loops below, so the write traps
+// refuse sandbox `set` / `defineProperty` on host `%TypedArray%.prototype`,
+// `%IteratorPrototype%`, `ArrayIterator.prototype`, etc.
+// `TypedArrayProto` — the shared `%TypedArray%.prototype` above every concrete
+// typed array (Buffer -> Uint8Array.prototype -> %TypedArray%.prototype).
+try {
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['TypedArray'] = Object.getPrototypeOf(Uint8Array.prototype);
+} catch (e) {}
+// The iterator prototype chain: concrete iterator prototypes and the shared
+// `%IteratorPrototype%` they all inherit from (GHSA-59g5 targets both).
+try {
+	const arrayIteratorProto = Object.getPrototypeOf([][Symbol.iterator]());
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['ArrayIterator'] = arrayIteratorProto;
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['IteratorPrototype'] = Object.getPrototypeOf(arrayIteratorProto);
+} catch (e) {}
+try {
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['StringIterator'] = Object.getPrototypeOf(''[Symbol.iterator]());
+} catch (e) {}
+try {
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['MapIterator'] = Object.getPrototypeOf(new Map()[Symbol.iterator]());
+} catch (e) {}
+try {
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['SetIterator'] = Object.getPrototypeOf(new Set()[Symbol.iterator]());
+} catch (e) {}
+try {
+	// %RegExpStringIteratorPrototype% — matchAll's iterator (Node 12+).
+	thisGlobalPrototypes['RegExpStringIterator'] = Object.getPrototypeOf('a'.matchAll(/a/g));
+} catch (e) {}
+
+// Keys in `thisGlobalPrototypes` that are NOT named globals (so the
+// globalsList/errorsList mapping loops below do not cover them) but MUST still
+// receive proto + identity mappings so their host prototypes are recognized
+// and their `constructor` reads collapse to the sandbox realm.
+const nonGlobalProtoKeys = [
+	'TypedArray',
+	'ArrayIterator',
+	'IteratorPrototype',
+	'StringIterator',
+	'MapIterator',
+	'SetIterator',
+	'RegExpStringIterator',
+];
 
 // Cache this-realm dangerous function constructors.
 // Used to block raw host Function constructors from leaking when handler
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the list of protected global objects to include all TypedArray and ArrayBuffer related intrinsics, as well as various iterator prototypes. It ensures these prototypes are properly mapped and their constructors are collapsed to the sandbox realm, preventing modification from within the sandbox.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 39 occurrences**

## GHSA-88hf-g992-jg85 · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox allowed an attacker to obtain raw host-realm prototype-reading functions (like `Object.prototype.__proto__` getter or `Object.getPrototypeOf`). By invoking these functions on a wrapped host object, the sandbox could pierce the flattened prototype chain enforced by the bridge, gaining access to intermediate host builtin prototypes (e.g., `EventEmitter.prototype`). These intermediate prototypes were not protected against modification, allowing the attacker to write a callable function onto them.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by installing a malicious function on a shared host prototype (e.g., `EventEmitter.prototype.emit = fn`). When a host-side operation later invoked this function with a host `this` context, the attacker's code would execute outside the sandbox with host privileges.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/bridge.js
+++ b/lib/bridge.js
@@ -1124,10 +1224,16 @@ function createBridge(otherInit, registerProxy) {
 		const protoDesc = otherSafeGetOwnPropertyDescriptor(otherGlobalPrototypes.Object, '__proto__');
 		if (protoDesc) {
 			addDangerousHostProtoMutator(protoDesc.set);
-			// Note: we intentionally do NOT add the getter — reading a host
-			// prototype is not, by itself, a privilege escalation primitive, and
-			// blocking the getter would break legitimate `instanceof` and
-			// inspection paths.
+			// SECURITY (GHSA-88hf-g992-jg85): classify the host `__proto__` GETTER
+			// as dangerous-to-DELIVER (not dangerous-to-invoke). Extracting the raw
+			// host getter and calling `gP.call(x)` pierces the bridge's flattened
+			// prototype view and hands the sandbox the true intermediate host
+			// builtin prototypes (EventEmitter.prototype, etc.), which are writable.
+			// We do NOT add it to the mutator set (which THROWS in the apply trap
+			// and would break legitimate `instanceof` / inspection that internally
+			// walk prototypes); instead we deny its DELIVERY at the read-side
+			// chokepoints so the sandbox can never hold the raw reader to invoke.
+			addDangerousHostProtoReader(protoDesc.get);
 		}
 		// Cache host `Function.prototype.call` / `Function.prototype.apply` as
 		// the canonical indirection primitives. The canonical PoC reaches host
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `dangerousHostProtoReaders` to identify and prevent the delivery of raw host prototype-reading functions into the sandbox. It also adds `hostObjectsUsedAsPrototype` and `looksLikeHostPrototype` to structurally identify and protect host prototype objects from having sandbox-controlled functions written to them, even if a future read path exposes them.

## GHSA-fcqc-726x-5wfc · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox allowed sandboxed code to access Node.js's shared Buffer pool. When a small Buffer was created, it would often be backed by a shared 64 KiB ArrayBuffer. The sandboxed code could then obtain a reference to this entire shared ArrayBuffer, allowing it to read and write memory outside its intended boundaries, including data from other host-realm buffers.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve a full sandbox escape, leading to arbitrary read and write access to the host-realm memory. This could result in information disclosure (e.g., reading secrets, database rows) and integrity compromise (e.g., corrupting host data), effectively breaking the isolation provided by the sandbox.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/setup-sandbox.js
+++ b/lib/setup-sandbox.js
@@ -584,7 +584,11 @@ class BufferHandler extends ReadOnlyHandler {
 			checkBufferAllocLimit(args[0]);
 			return LocalBuffer.alloc(args[0]);
 		}
-		return apply(LocalBuffer.from, LocalBuffer, args);
+		// SECURITY (GHSA-fcqc-726x-5wfc): deprecated Buffer(array|string|arrayBuffer)
+		// form aliases Buffer.from. Route through `bufferFrom` so the result is
+		// depooled (exact-size backing store) — a raw `LocalBuffer.from` here would
+		// return a pool-backed buffer whose `.buffer` exposes the shared 64 KiB pool.
+		return apply(bufferFrom, LocalBuffer, args);
 	}
 
 	construct(target, args, newTarget) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies Buffer creation methods (Buffer.from, Buffer.concat, Buffer.copyBytesFrom, and Buffer constructor aliases) within the sandbox to ensure that any buffer returned to sandboxed code owns its entire backing store. This is achieved by 'depooling' buffers that would otherwise be backed by Node's shared pool, copying their contents into a new, exact-size, non-pooled ArrayBuffer. This prevents sandboxed code from accessing the larger shared memory pool.

## GHSA-j3hm-6rg5-mchv · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The vm2 library, when configured with `require.external: true` but without an explicit `require.root`, allowed sandboxed code to use the host's `require()` function to load arbitrary paths. This effectively granted unrestricted access to the host filesystem and enabled full Remote Code Execution (RCE) because the sandboxed code could load and execute any module available to the host process.

### Impact

An attacker could escape the sandbox, execute arbitrary code on the host system with the privileges of the vm2 process, and potentially access or manipulate host files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/cli.js
+++ b/lib/cli.js
 		NodeVM.file(path, {
 			verbose: true,
 			require: {
-				external: true
+				external: true,
+				root: pa.dirname(path),
+				context: 'sandbox'
 			}
 		});
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces defense-in-depth measures. It prevents sandboxed code from requiring vm2's own package (which could lead to nested unrestricted sandboxes). For the CLI, it explicitly sets `require.root` to the script's directory and `context: 'sandbox'` to ensure external requires are sandboxed. It also adds a security warning when `require.external: true` is used without `require.root` in a host context.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 42 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Go, C#, PHP, Erlang, Python, Java, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-wjwh-qqvp-g4p4 · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox failed to properly isolate WebAssembly streaming compilation APIs. Specifically, `WebAssembly.compileStreaming` and `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming` could return Promises whose prototype chain reached the host realm's `Promise.prototype`. This bypasses the sandbox's `then`/`catch` overrides and `resetPromiseSpecies` mechanism, allowing an attacker to manipulate the `Symbol.species` property of the host Promise and execute arbitrary code in the host context.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve a complete sandbox escape, executing arbitrary code in the host environment with the privileges of the vm2 process. This leads to remote code execution (RCE) outside the sandbox.

### Key diff

```
- if (typeof WebAssembly.promising !== 'undefined') {
- 	localReflectDeleteProperty(WebAssembly, 'promising');
- }
+ if (typeof WebAssembly.compileStreaming !== 'undefined') {
+ 	localReflectDeleteProperty(WebAssembly, 'compileStreaming');
+ }
+ if (typeof WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming !== 'undefined') {
+ 	localReflectDeleteProperty(WebAssembly, 'instantiateStreaming');
+ }
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes `WebAssembly.compileStreaming` and `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming` from the sandbox environment. By removing these APIs, the sources of cross-realm-prototype Promises are eliminated, preventing attackers from leveraging the Promise species bypass to escape the sandbox.

## GHSA-p6vx-979v-rg4c · seroval

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→OBJECT_INJECTION`

### Root cause

The `seroval` library's `fromJSON()` function, specifically when deserializing Promise-related nodes, did not adequately validate the type of the resolved or rejected value. This allowed an attacker to inject a 'thenable' object (an object with a `then` method) into the deserialized Promise, which could then invoke arbitrary plugin-produced callables, bypassing a previous patch.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution or trigger unintended side effects by crafting a malicious serialized payload that, when deserialized, would cause the application to invoke attacker-controlled functions through the Promise resolution/rejection mechanism.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/seroval/src/core/context/deserializer.ts
+++ b/packages/seroval/src/core/context/deserializer.ts
@@ -571,6 +580,9 @@ function deserializePromise(
   const deferred = PROMISE_CONSTRUCTOR();
   const result = assignIndexedValue(ctx, node.i, deferred.p);
   const deserialized = deserialize(ctx, depth, node.f);
+  if (isThennable(deserialized)) {
+    throw new SerovalMalformedNodeError(node.f);
+  }
   if (node.s) {
     deferred.s(deserialized);
   } else {
@@ -629,33 +641,25 @@ function deserializePromiseConstructor(
   return value;
 }
 
-function deserializePromiseResolve(
+function deserializePromiseFulfill(
   ctx: DeserializerContext,
   depth: number,
-  node: SerovalPromiseResolveNode,
+  node: SerovalPromiseResolveNode | SerovalPromiseRejectNode,
 ): unknown {
   const deferred = ctx.base.refs.get(node.i) as
     | PromiseConstructorResolver
     | undefined;
   if (deferred) {
     validateNodeType(ctx, node, node.i, SerovalNodeType.PromiseConstructor);
-    deferred.s(deserialize(ctx, depth, node.a[1]));
-    return NIL;
-  }
-  throw new SerovalMissingInstanceError('Promise');
-}
-
-function deserializePromiseReject(
-  ctx: DeserializerContext,
-  depth: number,
-  node: SerovalPromiseRejectNode,
-): unknown {
-  const deferred = ctx.base.refs.get(node.i) as
-    | PromiseConstructorResolver
-    | undefined;
-  if (deferred) {
-    validateNodeType(ctx, node, node.i, SerovalNodeType.PromiseConstructor);
-    deferred.f(deserialize(ctx, depth, node.a[1]));
+    const deserialized = deserialize(ctx, depth, node.a[1]);
+    if (isThennable(deserialized)) {
+      throw new SerovalMalformedNodeError(node.a[1]);
+    }
+    if (node.t === SerovalNodeType.PromiseSuccess) {
+      deferred.s(deserialized);
+    } else {
+      deferred.f(deserialized);
+    }
     return NIL;
   }
   throw new SerovalMissingInstanceError('Promise');
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `isThennable` helper function to check if a value is an object with a callable `then` property. This check is then applied to the values being used to resolve or reject Promises during deserialization. If a 'thennable' value is detected in these contexts, a `SerovalMalformedNodeError` is thrown, preventing the invocation of untrusted callables.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 7 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, JavaScript, Python
> → GHSA-rmj7-2vxq-3g9f
> → GHSA-j3rv-43j4-c7qm
> → GHSA-9369-69wj-7m2f
> → GHSA-337j-9hxr-rhxg
> → GHSA-w23p-wrp7-ch38
> → GHSA-hp3v-5vw7-fx9w

## GHSA-rvm3-566m-v7fv · @capacitor/ios

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The Capacitor framework's internal HTTP proxy was designed to fetch arbitrary URLs as subresources (XHR/fetch) and return their content at the app's origin. However, the framework did not adequately restrict this proxy from being used for main frame navigations. This allowed an attacker to trick the WebView into loading remote content via the proxy path as a main frame, effectively rendering arbitrary external content at the app's origin.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution (RCE) by loading malicious JavaScript or HTML content at the app's origin. This content would then have full access to the Capacitor bridge, allowing it to execute native device functions and access sensitive data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/android/capacitor/src/main/java/com/getcapacitor/Bridge.java
+++ b/android/capacitor/src/main/java/com/getcapacitor/Bridge.java
@@ -408,6 +408,14 @@ public boolean launchIntent(Uri url) {
             return false;
         }
 
+        // The internal HTTP proxy fetches an arbitrary URL and returns its body at the app origin.
+        // It is only meant to be hit as an XHR/fetch subresource, which never reaches this method;
+        // a frame navigation here would render attacker content with full bridge access, so block it.
+        String path = url.getPath();
+        if (path != null && path.startsWith(CAPACITOR_HTTP_INTERCEPTOR_START)) {
+            return true;
+        }
+
         Uri appUri = Uri.parse(appUrl);
         if (
             !(appUri.getHost().equals(url.getHost()) && url.getScheme().equals(appUri.getScheme())) &&
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks to prevent the internal HTTP proxy path from being used for main frame navigations. In Android, `Bridge.java` now explicitly blocks `CAPACITOR_HTTP_INTERCEPTOR_START` paths from `launchIntent`. In `WebViewLocalServer.java`, the proxy is only served if `CapacitorHttp` is enabled and the request is not for the main frame. Similarly, in iOS, `WebViewDelegationHandler.swift` cancels navigations to `httpInterceptorStartIdentifier` paths.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 142 occurrences**

## GHSA-jqcg-44mw-7w3h · proxy-addr

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incorrect handling of IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses when determining if an IP address belonged to a trusted subnet. The `proxy-addr` library failed to properly canonicalize or compare these addresses, allowing an attacker to use an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address to bypass IP-based access controls that were intended for IPv4 addresses.

### Impact

An attacker could spoof their IP address, making the application believe the request originated from a trusted internal network. This could grant unauthorized access to sensitive resources or bypass security restrictions based on IP whitelisting.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -256,6 +256,13 @@ function trustMulti (subnets) {
     if (!isip(addr)) return false
 
     var ip = parseip(addr)
+
+    if (ip.kind() === 'ipv6' && ip.isIPv4MappedAddress()) {
+      // Canonicalize IPv4-mapped candidates to IPv4 so a mapped address
+      // cannot bypass the cross-family guard via the same-family path
+      ip = ip.toIPv4Address()
+    }
+
     var ipconv
     var kind = ip.kind()
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces canonicalization for IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses, converting them to their IPv4 equivalent before comparison. It also adds explicit checks to ensure that IPv6 subnets only match IPv4 addresses if they are specifically defined as IPv4-mapped subnets covering the `::ffff:` prefix, preventing cross-family bypasses.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 17 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, RUST, Erlang, Go
> → GHSA-g8f2-4f4f-5jqw
> → GHSA-429q-fhh4-r6hj
> → GHSA-6j2x-vhqr-qr7q
> → GHSA-7rqj-j65f-68wh
> → GHSA-mwr4-5g34-j5cq

## GHSA-jxw3-mjmx-3pqm · langflow

2026-10-05 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `WEAK_CRYPTO→RANDOM`

### Root cause

The application used Python's `random.seed()` with a secret key to generate a Fernet encryption key. The `random` module is a non-cryptographic pseudo-random number generator (PRNG), making the derived Fernet key predictable if the secret key was known or could be brute-forced, especially for short secret keys.

### Impact

An attacker could predict the Fernet encryption key if they knew or could guess the `SECRET_KEY`. This would allow them to decrypt sensitive information (e.g., API keys, global variables) stored by the application, leading to data exposure and potential unauthorized access or control.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/backend/base/langflow/services/auth/utils.py
+++ b/src/backend/base/langflow/services/auth/utils.py
@@ -329,9 +329,8 @@ def add_base64_padding(value: str) -> str:
     MINIMUM_KEY_LENGTH = 32  # noqa: N806
     if len(secret_key) < MINIMUM_KEY_LENGTH:
-        random.seed(secret_key)
-        key = bytes(random.getrandbits(8) for _ in range(32))
-        key = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(key)
+        digest = hashlib.sha256(secret_key.encode()).digest()  # 32 bytes
+        key = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(digest)
     else:
         key = add_base64_padding(secret_key).encode()
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the use of `random.seed()` and `random.getrandbits()` with `hashlib.sha256()` to derive the Fernet key from the secret key. This ensures that the key derivation is cryptographically secure and deterministic, preventing predictability for short secret keys.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 7 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Erlang, JavaScript, Go, Python
> → GHSA-cj8j-37rh-8475
> → GHSA-2528-jw5q-ww88
> → GHSA-v9gv-xp36-jgj8
> → GHSA-vh45-f885-3848
> → GHSA-rg76-677x-56q9
> → GHSA-w67g-5rqw-f597

## GHSA-x965-fc75-jpqh · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox's error sanitization mechanism, specifically for host-wrapped AggregateError and SuppressedError objects, had a flaw in its cycle detection. When an error object was rebuilt into a sandbox-realm equivalent, the `visited` map was not updated to point to the new, sanitized object. If the original host-wrapped error was encountered again during the sanitization of its sub-errors (e.g., if it was referenced multiple times), the cycle detection would return the *original*, unsanitized host-wrapped object, reintroducing host references into the sandbox.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious error object that, when processed by vm2, would allow a host-wrapped AggregateError or SuppressedError to bypass sanitization. This would lead to a sandbox escape, enabling the attacker to execute arbitrary code on the host system (RCE).

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/setup-sandbox.js
+++ b/lib/setup-sandbox.js
@@ -2099,9 +2099,23 @@ function handleException(e, visited) {
 	// and AggregateError (Node < 15), leaving the host-reference leak open
 	// precisely on the oldest supported versions.
 	if (!visited) visited = new LocalWeakMap();
-	// Cycle detection: if we've already visited this object, stop recursing
-	if (apply(localWeakMapGet, visited, [e])) return e;
-	apply(localWeakMapSet, visited, [e, true]);
+	const memoized = apply(localWeakMapGet, visited, [e]);
+	if (memoized) return memoized;
+	apply(localWeakMapSet, visited, [e, e]);
 	// SECURITY (GHSA-m283-3h24-438v): Error.cause is a free-form ES2022
 	// property that can carry an arbitrary host reference. When an embedder-
 	// exposed host function throws `new Error('msg', { cause: process })`,
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `handleException` function and the `sanitizeSuppressedError`/`sanitizeAggregateError` functions to correctly update the `visited` map. When a host-wrapped error is rebuilt into a sandbox-realm equivalent, the map entry for the original host object is updated to point to the new, sanitized sandbox object. Additionally, a new `_blockHostWrapped` function is introduced as a defense-in-depth measure to ensure that no host-wrapped objects are embedded into the sandbox-realm error structures.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 31 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, Python, PHP, JavaScript, Ruby, Erlang

## GHSA-gjq8-xm47-88rc · vm2

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 8.6 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

When a host function called from the sandbox returned a host Promise, the underlying host promise had no rejection handler attached on the host side. If the sandbox failed to attach its own handler in time, Node.js's default unhandledRejection policy would terminate the host process.

### Impact

A malicious actor within the vm2 sandbox could trigger a host process termination, leading to a denial of service for the application running vm2.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/bridge.js
+++ b/lib/bridge.js
@@ -2154,6 +2187,12 @@ function createBridge(otherInit, registerProxy) {
 			// enumerate the result through paths the bridge cannot intercept (e.g.
 			// a host method using an extracted host Reflect/Object reference).
 			stripDangerousSymbolsFromHostResult(ret);
+			// SECURITY (GHSA-gjq8-xm47-88rc): a host function invoked from the
+			// sandbox may return a host promise (e.g. an async host function, or
+			// events.once). Mark the underlying host promise handled so an
+			// ignored rejection cannot abort the host process. Idempotent and
+			// independent of the sandbox's own reaction — see markHostPromiseHandled.
+			markHostPromiseHandled(ret);
 			return thisFromOther(ret);
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `markHostPromiseHandled` function that attaches a benign no-op rejection handler to any host promise returned from a sandbox-invoked host function. This prevents Node.js from considering the promise unhandled and terminating the process, without interfering with the sandbox's own promise handling.

## GHSA-858h-whjf-mvg5 · simple-git

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stems from an incomplete fix for a previous command injection issue (CVE-2026-28291). The `simple-git` library failed to properly sanitize Git long-option abbreviations (e.g., `--receive-pack` abbreviated as `--receive-p`) when passed to Git commands. This allowed an attacker to bypass existing security measures.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the system where the `simple-git` application is running, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff is a `yarn` release file and does not contain the specific code changes for the `simple-git` library that address the vulnerability. It shows the deletion of a `yarn-4.14.1.cjs` file, which is not relevant to the `simple-git` package's internal logic.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff is for a `yarn` release file, not the `simple-git` package itself. Therefore, it does not directly show the fix for the `simple-git` vulnerability. The actual fix would involve more robust input validation and sanitization within `simple-git` to prevent Git long-option abbreviations from being interpreted as malicious commands.

## GHSA-g4wm-2vf7-vfgr · simple-git

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The simple-git library allowed Git configuration includes (e.g., `include.path`) to be used without proper blocking. This meant that if an attacker could control the Git configuration, they could inject arbitrary commands to be executed by Git.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on the system where simple-git is used, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/.yarn/releases/yarn-4.14.1.cjs
+++ /dev/null
deleted file mode 100755
index 7c010f0b..00000000
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the `yarn-4.14.1.cjs` file, which likely contained the vulnerable code or was part of a dependency that introduced the vulnerability. The fix for simple-git itself involves explicitly blocking Git configuration includes to prevent malicious command injection.

## GHSA-gcgf-fh7c-8gf2 · @orpc/zod

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `zodCoerceInternal` and `experimental_ZodSmartCoercionPlugin` functions, when coercing objects, created new objects using `{}`, which inherits from `Object.prototype`. When processing untrusted input keys, an attacker could supply keys like `__proto__` or `constructor.prototype` to modify the prototype of `Object`, leading to prototype pollution.

### Impact

An attacker could inject or modify properties on `Object.prototype`, potentially leading to denial of service, unexpected behavior, or even remote code execution in certain JavaScript environments if other parts of the application rely on vulnerable prototype properties.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/coercer.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/coercer.ts
-        const newObj: Record<string, unknown> = {}
+        const newObj: Record<string, unknown> = new NullProtoObj()
 
         const shape = schema_.shape
 
         const keys = new Set([
           ...Object.keys(value),
-          ...Object.keys(schema_.shape),
+          ...Object.keys(shape),
         ])
 
         for (const k of keys) {
           newObj[k] = zodCoerceInternal(
-            schema_.shape[k] ?? schema_._def.catchall,
+            (Object.hasOwn(shape, k) ? shape[k] : undefined) ?? schema_._def.catchall,
             value[k],
           )
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by replacing standard object creation (`{}`) with `new NullProtoObj()` for objects that process untrusted input keys. This ensures that the newly created objects do not inherit from `Object.prototype`, preventing prototype pollution. Additionally, it uses `Object.hasOwn` to safely check for properties on the schema's shape.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 39 occurrences**

## GHSA-4wwp-f6gw-6qm5 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-10-05 · Go · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→DIRECTORY_LISTING`

### Root cause

The application's file access control mechanism had an incomplete blocklist for sensitive files. While it blocked 'conf.json', it failed to include other critical configuration files, specifically those related to TLS private keys and certificates, allowing them to be read via the `getFile` API.

### Impact

An attacker could read TLS private keys and certificates, potentially leading to the decryption of HTTPS traffic, impersonation of the server, or other cryptographic attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/kernel/util/path_guard.go
+++ b/kernel/util/path_guard.go
@@ -25,14 +25,24 @@ import (
 
 // IsForbiddenAbsPath 判断绝对路径是否为敏感路径，HTTP 文件 API（kernel/api/file.go 的 refuseToAccess）
 // 与 MCP 文件工具（kernel/mcp/tools/file.go 的 resolvePath）共用同一黑名单：
-// conf/conf.json、data/snippets/conf.json、data/templates 目录以及 data/.siyuan/publishAccess.json。
+// conf 目录下的 conf.json 与 TLS 密钥材料、data/snippets/conf.json、data/templates 目录以及
+// data/.siyuan/publishAccess.json。
 func IsForbiddenAbsPath(abs string) bool {
 	fileNorm := NormalizeAndResolve(abs)
 
-	// 禁止访问配置文件 conf/conf.json（含 accessAuthCode/api.token/cookieKey 等明文凭据）
-	confPath := NormalizeAndResolve(filepath.Join(ConfDir, "conf.json"))
-	if fileNorm == confPath {
-		return true
+	// 禁止访问 conf 目录下的敏感文件：conf.json（含 accessAuthCode/api.token/cookieKey 等明文凭据）
+	// 以及 TLS 私钥与证书（见 GetOrCreateTLSCert，私钥被读取可导致 HTTPS 流量被解密或证书被伪造）
+	forbiddenConfFiles := []string{
+		"conf.json",
+		TLSCACertFilename,
+		TLSCAKeyFilename,
+		TLSCertFilename,
+		TLSKeyFilename,
+	}
+	for _, filename := range forbiddenConfFiles {
+		if fileNorm == NormalizeAndResolve(filepath.Join(ConfDir, filename)) {
+			return true
+		}
 	}
 
 	// 数据目录内的敏感位置（snippets/conf.json、templates、.siyuan/publishAccess.json），
```

### Fix summary

The patch expands the blocklist in the `IsForbiddenAbsPath` function to include all TLS-related private key and certificate files (TLSCACertFilename, TLSCAKeyFilename, TLSCertFilename, TLSKeyFilename) in addition to the existing 'conf.json'. This prevents unauthorized access to these sensitive files through the `getFile` API.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 7 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Go, PHP, C#
> → GHSA-2f7j-rp58-mr42
> → GHSA-g39v-cvjh-8fpf
> → GHSA-3jmg-p96m-m328
> → GHSA-rq7w-g337-39qq
> → GHSA-wf69-r4mx-43rr
> → GHSA-rxrh-4j9h-xgg9

## GHSA-5gmw-xhrv-c9v3 · tinypool

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `ThreadPool` class used a spread operator (`...`) to merge user-controlled `options` into its internal configuration. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary properties into the prototype chain of `Object`, leading to prototype pollution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by polluting the prototype of `Object` with malicious properties that are later used in the application's execution flow, specifically within the worker options.

### Key diff

```
-	this.options = { ...kDefaultOptions, ...options, filename, maxQueue: 0 }
+	this.options = Object.assign(
+	  Object.create(null),
+	  kDefaultOptions,
+	  options,
+	  { filename, maxQueue: 0 }
+	)
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes how the `this.options` object is initialized. Instead of using the spread operator, it now uses `Object.assign(Object.create(null), ...)`. This creates a new object with a null prototype, preventing user-controlled options from polluting the global `Object.prototype`.

## GHSA-85c8-ppgw-ccpr · tinypool

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The application was vulnerable to prototype pollution because it used `Object.assign` and spread syntax (`...`) to merge user-controlled options into an object without first ensuring that the target object had a null prototype. This allowed an attacker to inject properties into `Object.prototype` via the `options` object, which would then affect all objects in the application.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by polluting the prototype with specific properties that are later used in the application's execution flow, such as modifying worker options or other critical configurations.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/index.ts
+++ b/src/index.ts
@@ -656,12 +657,12 @@ class ThreadPool {
       ? maybeFileURLToPath(options.filename)
       : null
 
-    this.options = Object.assign(
-      Object.create(null),
-      kDefaultOptions,
-      options,
-      { filename, maxQueue: 0 }
-    )
+    this.options = withNullPrototype({
+      ...kDefaultOptions,
+      ...options,
+      filename,
+      maxQueue: 0,
+    })
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function `withNullPrototype` which creates a new object with a null prototype and then copies the source object's own properties onto it. This function is now used when initializing `ThreadPool` and `Tinypool` options, as well as when processing `run` and `recycleWorkers` options, preventing prototype pollution by ensuring that user-controlled input cannot modify `Object.prototype`.

## GHSA-v5rq-49vh-5v5c · @simple-git/argv-parser

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `simple-git` library's `argv-parser` component failed to include the `VISUAL` environment variable in its list of potentially unsafe editor configurations. This omission meant that commands specified via the `VISUAL` environment variable were not subject to the same security checks as other editor-related environment variables, allowing for arbitrary command execution.

### Impact

An attacker could inject and execute arbitrary commands on the system where the `simple-git` application is running by manipulating the `VISUAL` environment variable, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/argv-parser/src/env/parse-env.ts
+++ b/packages/argv-parser/src/env/parse-env.ts
@@ -22,6 +22,7 @@ const GitEnvKeys = {
    'pager': 'allowUnsafePager',
    'prefix': 'allowUnsafeConfigPaths',
    'ssh_askpass': 'allowUnsafeAskPass',
+   'visual': 'allowUnsafeEditor',
 } as const satisfies Record<string, VulnerabilityCategory>;
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds `visual` to the `GitEnvKeys` constant, mapping it to `allowUnsafeEditor`. This ensures that the `VISUAL` environment variable is now correctly identified and handled as a potentially unsafe editor configuration, subjecting its value to necessary security checks to prevent command injection.

## GHSA-x6jw-m9v5-85vh · simple-git

2026-10-05 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `simple-git` library's `unsafe-operation` guard did not correctly identify and block all configurations that could lead to arbitrary command execution. Specifically, it missed the `trailer.cmd` and `trailer.command` Git configurations, as well as the `-x` or `--exec` flags for the `rebase` command, which allow an attacker to specify and execute arbitrary commands.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary commands to be executed on the system where the `simple-git` application is running, potentially leading to remote code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/argv-parser/src/vulnerabilities/detect-vulnerable-config-writes.ts
+++ b/packages/argv-parser/src/vulnerabilities/detect-vulnerable-config-writes.ts
@@ -68,5 +68,7 @@ const preventUnsafeConfig = [
    preventConfigBuilder('sequence.editor', 'allowUnsafeEditor'),
    preventExpandedConfigBuilder('submodule.update', 'allowUnsafeSubmodule'),
    preventExpandedConfigBuilder('tar.command', 'allowUnsafeCommandBinaries'),
+   preventExpandedConfigBuilder('trailer.cmd', 'allowUnsafeCommandBinaries'),
+   preventExpandedConfigBuilder('trailer.command', 'allowUnsafeCommandBinaries'),
    preventExpandedConfigBuilder('url.insteadOf', 'allowUnsafeUrlRewrite'),
 ];

--- a/packages/argv-parser/src/vulnerabilities/detect-vulnerable-flags.ts
+++ b/packages/argv-parser/src/vulnerabilities/detect-vulnerable-flags.ts
@@ -69,6 +69,8 @@ const preventUnsafeFlags = [
    preventFlagBuilder('clone', /^-\w*u/, 'allowUnsafePack'),
    preventFlagBuilder('clone', '--u', 'allowUnsafePack'),
    preventFlagBuilder('push', /^--exec$/, 'allowUnsafePack', { name: '--exec' }),
+   // `git` accepts unambiguous abbreviations of long options, so `--ex` and `--exe` are `--exec`
+   preventFlagBuilder('rebase', /^(-x|--ex(ec?)?)$/, 'allowUnsafeExec', { name: '-x or --exec' }),
    preventFlagBuilder(null, '--template', 'allowUnsafeTemplateDir'),
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds `trailer.cmd` and `trailer.command` to the list of configurations that are prevented from being written unsafely. It also adds a new `preventFlagBuilder` for the `rebase` command to block the `-x` and `--exec` flags, which can be used to execute arbitrary commands during a rebase operation.
